ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u4	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! In 2012, the Polish Parliament ratified the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities. It is one of the pillars of the United Nations human rights system and has been a party to it since 2010. The Convention is an extremely important document for the environment of people with disabilities, as it gives hope for full participation in public, economic, social and professional life of people with disabilities. The Polish State in 2012 gave an important signal that it is considered a country fully ready to fulfil the numerous commitments concerning the guarantee of the rights of persons with disabilities contained in this document. Poland has committed itself, among others. to establish: cells in government structures for matters related to the implementation of the Convention, government coordination mechanism, national action programme, independent mechanism for promoting, protecting and monitoring implementation activities in the field of the Convention. Over 3 years have passed since 2012. Minister, I have a question. Please tell us at what stage the implementation of this Convention is at. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u5	Minister Krzysztof Michalkiewicz will speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, the question is important in various ways. One element worth highlighting is that this year has been 10 years since the UN prepared the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities. She pointed out that more than three years ago this convention was also ratified by Poland, it can be said that almost 4 years. The next year will be the 5th anniversary of its ratification by Poland and it will certainly be a good time to sum up and assess the functioning of the Convention. By ratifying the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, Poland also stated that most of the articles, most of the rights contained therein, are adequately reflected in the legal system of our country. We've only introduced two exceptions here. It's an art. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 and Article 4 Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 (a) (i) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) As a result, Poland has benefited from the possibility provided for in the Treaties' law to raise objections and to make an interpretation declaration. The ratification of the Convention was possible because a number of amendments to the law were made. The application of all other articles, the provisions of the Convention is therefore guaranteed in Polish law. As you said, a team was set up under the leadership of a government representative for persons with disabilities, who is responsible for implementing the provisions of the Convention when it comes to government administration. There are representatives of various resorts in this team. Ten meetings of this band have already been held. There we raised a number of issues important for the implementation of the Convention. Recently, two weeks ago, there was a 10th meeting of the team - it was 21 June - and at this meeting we dealt with the availability of Internet services when it comes to people with disabilities. The subject is very important, as digital exclusion has specific consequences for many groups of people with disabilities. Access to the Internet, access to digital information offers an opportunity to participate in social life, to communicate, to contact and to participate. At this meeting there were representatives of the Visual Foundation, who presented the "Availability Report 2016" on the application of the accessibility standard by public administration websites and conducted a workshop for web editors, where the software used by people with various disabilities was presented, most commonly found on websites errors and methods of avoiding them. The important thing is that progress could be seen in this area. Of 118 services audited 22% received a higher than sufficient assessment. This is still the beginning of the road, but I will remind you that in the audit carried out by the Visual Foundation in 2013, the percentage of parties that could be considered as available was 1,7%. , in 2015 - 12.8%. As I am saying, at the moment 22% of the websites run by the administration have been assessed as good or very good. As part of the meeting of this team - I am talking about this longer, because I believe that it is digital exclusion or digital inclusion, in order to put it positively, it is extremely, extremely important - the proposal was made to set up a special subcommittee that will take care of it, so that all websites are accessible to people with disabilities and that the errors that occur in them are eliminated. Of course, it is very important to monitor the implementation of the Convention by the environment of people with disabilities. In fact, what is important at the moment is a project that concerns it: implementing the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities - a common matter. This project will be implemented between March 2016 and February 2018. The result of this report is the identification of legal, administrative, organisational barriers to the implementation of public policy conventions, as well as recommendations for the desired directions of action. As I mentioned, the project is fully implemented by the environment of organisations that bring together people with disabilities. She wears, among other things. consultations in all voivodships. We hope that this will be a strong voice showing at what stage we are in the functioning of the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities in our country. As I say, the convention on the legal side is guaranteed. Changes in the law have been made. However, it is really about society as a whole, about government administration, but also about local government, about the whole sector of non-governmental organisations. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Only with joint action can we guarantee that the Convention will be respected and implemented. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u23	Thank you. I have another question about this amount. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u24	It needs to be developed. The answer to this question needs to be developed. So, yes, we have to remember that we are talking about benefits that are based on no longer binding provisions. This regulation, which gave rise to the granting of such a pension, was repealed from the date of the start of the pension reform, namely on 1 January 1999, so we are in fact dealing with persons whose powers were fixed by decision of the pension authority before 1 January 1999. In fact, as we look at the legal situation, we will see that we are dealing with a pension which is life-long, i.e. that these are life-long entitlements, rights that belong to insured persons who receive these benefits, regardless of the continuation of child care. Once this right has been determined by the decision of the pension authority, this condition, which was the personal care of the child included in the I or II disability group, because so these conditions were defined, already, can be said, does not matter at the moment. So these are actually all the people who take this witness, whether or not this need for child care still exists.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u25	Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u26	I can still do that, right? Thank you, but I'll clarify that question. So it can be assumed that those amounts that the Minister gave were estimated amounts and that there was no obligation to verify. Let me give you an example. The point is that if someone had acquired this benefit before the child was 16 years old - and then there was a current ruling - all the time when that law was in force, there was no obligation to verify this, i.e. to stand before the committee again and report another decision to the Social Insurance Institution after 16 years of age, which confirmed this disability.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u27	There is no legal basis for this type of verification procedure to be carried out because there is no such regulation. This regulation was, so to speak, removed from legal trading on 1 January 1999. But, of course, as far as the background is concerned - because I guess the question goes to the problem that arose after the changes in the Family Benefits Act, where the care provision for parents resigning from employment was increased in order to take personal care of a disabled child whose disability was essentially created before the age of 18 - it is evident that there is a problem. This is also a problem which arose after the Constitutional Tribunal issued a ruling recognising that the rights of parents who have custody of persons whose disability arose during childhood and those who have cared for persons whose disability arose at an adult age infringe the Constitution. This is a non-constitutional situation. This incompatibility with the constitution lies, you can say, in different terms of authority. We know that the benefits of this first group of people are higher, 1300 PLN, and the benefits of those who are caring for those who have an adult disability are much lower, and this is the amount of PLN 520. We are dealing with unequal treatment. As we look from this perspective at those pensions which were granted until the end of 1998 on the basis of the regulation we are talking about, we see that we are also dealing with such, let's say, unequal treatment, because, as we look in turn at the grounds for acquiring the right to provide care in the amount of PLN 1300, we see that one of the negative conditions excluding the right to this benefit is having a fixed right to retirement, any pension, including a pension granted under that regulation. In short, it can be said that those who have a decision granting such a benefit cannot apply for a care provision of PLN 1300. This is a problem that requires a systematic, comprehensive solution, and we are working on this solution.

As I have already mentioned, this process will begin with the notification submitted by the Government of the United Kingdom to the European Council, which is the time to prepare guidelines and also to formulate the Polish interest, which will be adopted unanimously by the European Council, because after this notification the European Council will issue guidelines on the principles and objectives of these negotiations, define the objectives, process and select the negotiators. It should also be assumed, from such a practical point of view, that the subject of negotiations on the United Kingdom exit agreement will be a permanent subject of European Council meetings. The next step will be to make recommendations by the Commission on the negotiations to the Council of the European Union, i.e. pursuant to Article. 218 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, the Council shall adopt a decision authorising negotiations and appoint a negotiator. The Agreement on the exit of the United Kingdom from the European Union shall be concluded by the Council on behalf of the European Union by a qualified majority of at least 20 of the 27 Member States representing at least 60% of the population of the European Union. It is also worth noting that the European Parliament is not formally involved in the negotiation process. Of course, it has the right to receive information on this process, but its agreement, which will be expressed by a majority, is necessary for the Council to conclude an exit agreement with the United Kingdom from the European Union. It is also worth noting that there is a double process here, i.e. until the conclusion of a new agreement on relations between the United Kingdom - the European Union needs to be included in the exit agreement. transitional provisions, pending the conclusion of this final agreement. This second stage will be the conclusion of an agreement on new relations between the United Kingdom and the European Union, and negotiations on this matter will only be started when the agreement I mentioned earlier was adopted. Then these negotiations will be conducted in such a way that the United Kingdom, the United Kingdom, will be treated as a third country. Of course, here is the question of whether this is an agreement that will be in the exclusive competence of the European Union. This will probably be a mixed agreement, which will also bring together the competences of the Member States, which means that a mixed agreement, will also require the approval of individual Member States in the ratification procedure. She referred to the rights of Polish citizens, workers on the British market. We will urge that the rights of Polish citizens be guaranteed on the British market. Take a look at these negotiations also in the context of the presence of British citizens in the various Member States of the European Union.

But what I can say is that the decision was actually made by the KNF or an institution independent of the Minister of Finance based on an analysis of the economic situation of the Wielkopolska SKOK. As far as we know, the decision was indeed based on the 2013 financial statements, but this is explained in such a way that the Supreme Court failed to submit further financial statements for the following years, i.e. that the last report approved by the statutory auditor is simply a 2013 report, is not for 2014, is not for 2015. This is the fact that this report for 2013 is the basis for the KNF to introduce the committee board. The reported year 2013 is more factual in the sense that it simply does not exist, i.e. We also looked for information about the financial statements for the following years, but we did not find such information that the SKOK would present the financial statements for the following years. By decision of 17 May 2016, the Commission pursuant to Article. 73. The 1st Act on the SKOK has just established a district administrator. The analysis of the situation on the basis of the financial reporting of the results of the inspection showed that there is a threat, according to the KNF, of stopping the payment of liabilities by the Wielkopolska SKOK. And we were informed that from 17 June to 12 July 2013 the employees of the KNF office carried out a comprehensive inspection of the Wielkopolska SKOK as at 31 March 2013. This audit, in the opinion of the KNF, showed that the economic and financial situation of the cash register was unsatisfactory. There have been numerous irregularities, weaknesses in the management process, in particular credit risk. These irregularities were a threat to the security of the cash collected in the cash register accounts. And this is the information presented by KNF. The level of the cashier's own funds was insufficient, did not support the business and cover the risks incurred, did not ensure that the solvency ratio was maintained at the minimum required level of law. The financial result was not a source of capital growth. As of 31 March 2013, the cashier showed a net loss and the control activities indicated the need to create additional write-downs to the value held by the cashier. The Commission received information as a result of supervisory activities, inter alia. the incorrect valuation of some of the cashier's assets and the auditor carrying out the audit of the financial statements for 2013 at the request of the cashier failed to carry out a detailed analysis of these issues. Therefore, KNF, when exercising its statutory right, pursuant to Article. 62d paragraph. 2 of the Act on the SKOK requested the audit of the financial statements for 2013. As a result of the audit of the approved financial statements of the cashier for 2013, the statutory auditor identified relevant facts affecting the assessment of the correctness and reliability of the financial statements of the cashier. The inclusion of the audit auditor identified for the purpose of the cash register, the adjustment committee in the financial statements resulted in negative own funds, negative financial result and solvency ratio. The statutory board of directors of the cash register, such as the separation of the organised part of the company, which took place twice in 2012 and 2013, as well as the sale of outstanding claims, which took place in 2012, 2014 and 2015, did not improve the situation of the cashier and in addition put the cash register at risk of costs, among others. on the sale of a trade mark and the levying of licence fees. The cashier's situation continued to deteriorate. From mid-2014 until the end of 2015, the cash register showed losses. The analysis of the cashier's reporting data submitted to UKNF also indicated that the cashier did not correctly include in the accounts and financial statements of the transfers to the opened companies in exchange for the transferred shares until the date of the decision. In the KNF's view, the cash register management process was inadequate, ineffective and ineffective. The separation by the cashier in 2012 and 2013 of the organised part of the company was a one-off action and did not affect the entity's stable situation. One-off actions did not demonstrate the effectiveness and stability of the cash register. They only resulted in an improvement in the presentation layer, but obscuring the actual situation of the cash register. Returning to the very essence of the question, I would like to inform you that the agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the Russian Federation on the principles of local border traffic of 14 December 2011 provides for the possibility of suspending the application of the agreement in whole or in part because of the protection of State security, public policy or the protection of public health. At the same time, this agreement does not provide for an intergovernmental consultation procedure for the intention to suspend this local border traffic, but provides that the suspension decision should be notified by diplomatic means to the other party no later than 48 hours. before it is implemented. This condition has been met as the suspension of the application of Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 ww. the Polish-Russian agreement, as well as the suspension of the application of the article. 3 (1) (a) 1 Polish-Ukrainian agreement on local border traffic rules in the part concerning the entry and stay in the territory of the Republic of Poland of residents of the border area, Russia and Ukraine respectively, with permissions issued by the competent authorities of the Republic of Poland, lasts from 4 July 2016 until the date of cessation of the reasons for suspension. Because the reasons for the suspension were not Although mentioned at the time of the adoption of this decision, they relate closely to safety issues, risk assessment. They were the basis of the government's decision, because the information that reached the government in this matter demonstrated that there was a certain and growing danger, above all, related to the great events that take place this month, namely, firstly, the NATO Summit and World Youth Day, accompanied by the visit of His Holiness the Holy Father Francis. The measures taken are preventive and will provide even more complete control over people arriving in Poland. This will enable people to be monitored more effectively, preventing the influx of undesirable people, without hindering the movement of travellers for good intentions at the same time. The measures taken in this respect must not be subject to economic interest, because the safety issue here is absolutely in the first place, in particular if the threat of this safety is to be universal. The existence of negative economic effects, particularly in the short term, does not constitute an argument justifying a reduction in measures aimed at ensuring the smooth and efficient functioning of the State in the area of security in the long term. After all, this is about the lives of Polish citizens. Thank you very much.

The leader of the team is Professor Józef Hrynkiewicz. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u86	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Minister! Because in every country, just not in ours, laboratory diagnostics is the eye in the head, because 80% Medical diagnosis is based on laboratory tests. Modern laboratory diagnostics, due to their complexity, require close cooperation of the physician with laboratory diagnostics. According to data, the maintenance costs of hospital laboratories in Poland vary between 3% a 5% the hospital budget, which shows that the quality of the laboratory examination affects the proper disposal of the entire cost of treatment. The lack of legislation obliging medicinal entities to have medical diagnostic laboratories in their structure leads to the liquidation of Polish laboratories and the transfer of laboratory diagnostic services to external companies without real public supervision, and these can be treated as a non-medical service, such as laundry, kitchen or cleaning. In addition, the lack of evaluation of the laboratory test results in hospital management viewing the medical diagnostic laboratory only on the cost side and not as a profit for the hospital and patients. Possession by the hospital of a medical diagnostic laboratory [[♪ The bell ♪]] it will ensure that diagnostic examinations carried out under the hospital contract with the insurer will be carried out in due time and in the highest quality, as well as the cooperation of laboratory diagnostics with hospital doctors in the diagnosis and treatment of the patient will be ensured.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u87	Thank you, Member. And please respond to the Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u88	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In answer to Mrs Sobecka's question, I would like to say that according to the provisions of the Law of 27 July 2001 on medical diagnostics, the diagnostic laboratory is a medical operator and operates alone or is an organizational unit, which is part of the company, e.g. hospital. In any case, the medical provider shall decide on its organisational structure also in this respect. Therefore, the current rules do not prohibit a medicinal entity from performing a medical activity such as hospital treatment to have such a laboratory in its organisational structure. It is important in the context of ensuring the quality and safety of the tests that all laboratories wishing to operate in accordance with the profile are required to comply with the requirements laid down in the Regulation of the Minister for Health of 3 March 2004 on the requirements to be complied with by the medical diagnostic laboratory and the standards resulting from the Regulation of the Minister for Health of 23 March 2006 on quality standards for medical diagnostic and microbiological laboratories. Standards for these standards are designed to ensure an adequate level of quality of laboratory diagnostic activities, thus achieving the necessary acceptable level of patient and staff safety and achieving reliable laboratory results. This is the case for the proper organisation of laboratory work and responsibility of the laboratory manager and the medical entity, which does not exclude the personal responsibility of the diagnostic officer in carrying out laboratory diagnostic activities. In 2009 laboratories were obliged to participate permanently in the basic external quality evaluation programmes organised by the Central Centre for Quality Research in Microbiological Diagnostics in Warsaw and the Central Centre for Quality Research in Laboratory Diagnostics in Łódź. These are the bodies subordinate to the Minister of Health. In the case of studies not covered by the basic programmes, the laboratory may participate in other national or international programmes. According to the Law on Laboratory Diagnostics, the National Council of Laboratory Diagnostics is empowered to check and evaluate the performance of laboratory diagnostics. The inspections shall be carried out and evaluated by the visitors appointed by the National Council of Laboratory Diagnostics. If irregularities affecting the results of the diagnostic tests are found, the inspector shall inform the competent voivode. In order to strengthen the supervision of quality control of research, it is planned to establish a team to develop the concept of changes in the functioning of quality testing centres in laboratory and microbiological diagnostics involving all interested parties, i.e. Minister of Health, Central Centre for Quality Research in Laboratory Diagnostics, Central Centre for Quality Research in Microbiological Diagnostics, National Chamber of Laboratory Diagnostics, General Chamber of Medicine, Polish Society for Laboratory Diagnostics, College of Laboratory Medicine and National Health Fund chaired by a national consultant in the field of laboratory diagnostics. The aim of the team will be: to develop legal solutions that will enable the quality of laboratory tests to be enforced, to publish on the websites of quality control centres obtained by laboratories, to introduce a mandatory process of corrective actions to achieve correct results; to increase the powers of the Central Centre for Quality Research in Laboratory Diagnostics in Łódź and the Central Centre for Quality Research in Microbiological Diagnostics in Warsaw, which will consist in establishing criteria for the approval or retention of laboratory testing by laboratories on the basis of results obtained from inter-laboratory testing tests. The extension of the scope of research covered by the controls organised by these bodies will also fall within the scope of this team's work. It should also be clarified that the accreditation process for medical diagnostic laboratories has been in progress since 2009. Polish Accreditation Center. In the area of operation of medical laboratories, two standards are currently applicable: ISO: PN-EN ISO 17025 According to the data contained in the Register of Medical Entities 543 hospitals have laboratory diagnostic laboratories (laboratorium resort code 7100) in their organizational structure at 1005 of all hospitals; 193 hospitals with more than 50 beds do not have a laboratory structure, including 22 hospitals with more than 500 beds, 63 hospitals with 201.500 beds and 108 hospitals with 51.200 beds. Thank you very much, Marshal.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u89	Thank you very much, Minister. And I would ask you to ask an additional question to Anna Sobecka.

Poland ratified the above mentioned paragraph. 2012 Convention Unfortunately, we are the only country from a block of post-communist countries that has not prepared such a strategy. As a result, no knowledge of what this deinstitutionalisation process is and how it should be carried out using the European Union's resources has been communicated. The consequence of that decision is that the European funds to support this process, i.e. at least 5 billion zlotys, have been poorly programmed. Moreover, the criteria for selecting projects from different competitions are currently under development. These criteria are not linked to one strategic document, a deinstitutionalisation strategy. The funds thus obtained from European funds could powerfully support the reform of psychiatry, which is very necessary and necessary. The question is, is the Minister considering setting up an inter-ministerial team for the deinstitutionalisation process? There are three departments: health, family, work and social policy and development. Is the Ministry of Health planning to prepare a deinstitutionalisation strategy that would allow European funds to be applied for precisely this purpose? Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u100	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! At the moment, the draft regulation of the Council of Ministers on the National Mental Health Programme was forwarded to the Chairman of the Government's Programming Team asking for approval and inclusion in the list of legislative and programme work of the Council of Ministers. At the time of acceptance, this draft will be addressed without delay to the agreement, opinion and public consultation with a 21-day deadline for comments. With regard to the question of including the reform of psychiatry in the National Reform Programme, I would like to explain that mental health measures have been included in the Ministry of Development's document entitled: The strategy for responsible development is intended to replace the country's current medium-term development strategy. To date, they have been developed with the participation of representatives of the resorts and the voivodship centers of the foundation for the above mentioned. strategies, which will be held in July-September this year. the subject of a wide public and inter-ministerial consultation. The content of the assumptions emphasizes that the consequence of mental state is often loss of work, increased sense of exclusion and helplessness, isolation and economic dependence on the state. Unfortunately, the lack of system solutions based on the model of environmental treatment of mental disorders and the coordination of policies at the interface of mental health and social security cause many people to suffer from mental disorders, gradually excluding themselves from the labour market, feeding on a number of inactive people. The strategic actions envisaged for implementation in this area include the implementation of the "Model for the provision of services to persons with mental disorders and diseases" project - the development and pilot testing of various approaches to the development of an organisational structure to be coordinated, including medical and social support networks for persons during and after mental crises. This is the completion of the solutions provided for in the National Mental Health Programme, which aims to create a system of assistance for people after a mental crisis in their local environment without the need for hospitalisation. One of the components of this programme is also expected to be the implementation of the actions foreseen in the National Mental Health Programme, which considers it particularly important to promote the environmental model of psychiatric health care, inter alia. by stimulating the development of mental health centres, the development of personnel involved in the care of persons with mental disorders and the development of general rules of conduct for the representation of persons with mental disorders in the media and the conduct of information and education activities concerning the need to respect the rights of persons with mental disorders. Actions to prevent mental health problems and improve mental well-being will be part of the 2016-2020 National Health Programme, as well as under the 2016-2020 Programme for the Prevention of Depression in Poland. It should be noted that on 30 June, the Ministry of Health announced a competition for the selection of the programme's programme's developer. Amount allocated to the implementation of the tasks [[♪ The bell ♪]] indicated in the abovementioned section. The announcement is 200,000. zł. The National Mental Health Programme and the National Health Programme for 2016 and 2020 are part of the strategic draft of the Minister of Health submitted for the SOR strategy. The Government's draft Print Act No 600 entered the Sejm on 6 June 2016. The first reading of the draft law took place at the 21st meeting of the Sejm on 21 June this year. Sejm, based on art. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed the above draft to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs for consideration. On 22 June the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy addressed this project to the Permanent Sub-Committee on Labour Market Affairs. On 23 June, the subcommittee, considering the draft law, adopted nine amendments, which mostly aimed at defining more precisely the terms of the proposed law. I will allow myself, on behalf of the Committee on Social Policy and the Family, to inform the High House of some of them. Amendment 2 proposes clarification of the proposed Article. Article 8a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 1 in such a way that it is clear from the wording of this provision that the amount of this remuneration should be determined in a contract in such a way as to guarantee the right of the person concerned to pay for each hour of work of an amount not less than the minimum hourly rate. In turn, Amendment 6 to Article. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 is intended to clarify the provision in such a way as to make it clear that the initiation of a public procurement procedure was carried out under the provisions of the Public Procurement Law as it stood before 1 September 2016. An important amendment included in the draft law is the deletion of the current article. Article 6 (6) (a) 2 and 3 of the Law of 10 October 2002 on minimum remuneration for work. According to the provision of this Article, the remuneration of the employee during his first year of work could not be less than 80% minimum wage for work. At this point, it should be noted that this exceptionally discriminatory provision was created during a difficult labour market situation, with the aim of improving the labour market situation especially for young people. This regulation, as I have already mentioned, took place during a period of high unemployment and was treated as a sub-minimum. Today, for about 2 years, we have seen improvements in the labour market. Today, the market is looking for an employee. Also, the European Commission in the update of the forecast for economic developments in our country indicates that GDP growth in 2016 will be 3.5%. The positive trend in the labour market is also confirmed by the survey of the economic activity of the population, which indicates that the last 2 years have been a period of rapid growth in the employment rate. In view of the above, the provision that for the first year of the worker's work his remuneration is 80% has been deleted. the minimum remuneration, as this is an exceptionally discriminatory provision, and given the current economic circumstances, it is deeply unjustified. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The subcommittee, considering the amendments tabled, adopted them unanimously and agreed that the government party should clarify its position in two cases until the meeting of the Committee on Social Policy and the Family. At the meeting of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy held on 5 July this year, the applicant presented two amendments to the committee. And yes, in Amendment 1 in Article. 7 as amended 7 in Article. 8d, presented by the government party, seeks to specify more precisely the cases in which the contract of order or service will not be covered by the proposed law. The applicant here referred to cases where the receiving or providing services determines the place and time of execution of the order or service and at the same time has a commission remuneration. In order to avoid interpretation doubts when applying the proposed provision, it is proposed to define the concept of commission remuneration in the new paragraph. Article 3 8d. The same amendment proposes the inclusion in a separate editorial unit - in the added paragraph. 2 - the definition of the term "day', which occurs three times in Article. 8d. In Amendment 2 in Article. 14 It is proposed that the Act will enter into force on 1 January 2017, with the exception of: first, Article. 1 point (1) (a) a, which refers to the definition of the concept of "minimum hourly rate' and to the concept of "acceptor' or "service provider'; secondly, Article. 7, which sets out the valuer mechanism for determining the minimum hourly rate to be applied in 2017, and sets the deadline for announcing the rate in the Polish Monitor by 30 September 2016. In both cases, the provisions are to enter into force on the day of the announcement. It should be stressed that the earlier implementation of the Article. 7 of the proposed law, i.e. from the date of publication and not from 1 September this year, will enable the President of the Council of Ministers to issue a notice containing information on the minimum hourly rate in force in 2017. This will be quite important for the parties to order contracts or service contracts, which will already know the amount of this rate and will be able from 1 September this year. benefit from the provisions of Article. The proposed law will, in turn, enable the parties to negotiate an agreement on an appropriate change of remuneration. Dear Marshal, Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! The first is to refrain from establishing the employee's salary in the first year of his work at 80%. minimum wage for work. The second amendment is to exclude the allowance for night work in the calculation of the minimum wage. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u280	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the draft Act on the ratification of the Protocol to the North Atlantic Treaty on Montenegro's accession, signed in Brussels on 19 May 2016. The Act, which the Law and Justice Club supports, agrees that the President of the Republic of Poland ratifys the Protocol to the North Atlantic Treaty. The decision to accept Montenegro to NATO was taken by NATO Foreign Ministers on 2 December 2014. It is an expression of strengthening and unifying the European continent. It is a continuation of an open-door policy supported by law and justice. As a consequence of Montenegro's accession, NATO will extend its security guarantees to the next Western Balkan country, thus expanding the area of security and stability in Europe and reducing Russian influence in this region of Europe. Montenegro's membership in NATO will not only contribute to the country's security but will also be fundamental to the stabilisation of the whole Western Balkans region. Moreover, it is of major strategic importance that the Alliance will control the entire coastline of the Adriatic Sea basin from the moment of Montenegro's accession to NATO. Although it is a small country and only 10 years ago it gained independence, it has made a huge reform effort in the political, economic and security and defence sphere. Montenegro's armed forces have sufficient capacity to adapt efficiently to the requirements of allies. Montenegro sees Poland as the leader of the region of Central Europe and its ally, as confirmed by Minister Witold Waszczykowski's visit to Podgorica in May this year. The Montenegrin Party has repeatedly signaled that it wants Poland as the host of the NATO Summit and a country that strongly supports the invitation of this country to the Alliance to be one of the first countries to ratify the accession protocol. The rapid ratification of the Protocol and the adoption of Montenegro to NATO will effectively close the discussion on Russia's possible destabilising action towards this country. The entry into force of the protocol will not have social, economic, financial or legal consequences for Poland and does not require additional sources of funding. However, it will have a positive geopolitical and international impact on our country due to the further strengthening and expansion of the area of stability and military cooperation between the countries of this region of Europe. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of this law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u296	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As rapporteur, I would like to present a report by the Committee on Physical Culture, Sports and Tourism on the draft law amending the Sports Act. The Commission discussed the project during the previous meeting of the Sejm, i.e. on 23 June 2016 Following the first reading of the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism, following the adoption and adoption of certain amendments proposed, among others. through the Legislative Office brings to the Sejm the attached bill. The report, which came to me to present to the High Sejm, concerns the work carried out on the above mentioned. a draft law which provides for the amendment of certain provisions of the Act of 25 June 2010 on Sport, OJ No. U. z 2016 poz. 176, concerning the fight against doping in sport, contained above all in Chapter 9 of the Act. The proposed provisions aim primarily at ordering and regulating national arrangements for the establishment and implementation of disciplinary rules on doping in sport in a manner consistent with the principles of the World Anti-Doping Code. In order to comply with the principles of the Code, Poland is obliged by art. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 of the International Convention against Doping in Sport, drawn up in Paris on 19 October 2005 Let me just remind you that the Convention is a comprehensive international agreement aimed at harmonising action to combat the use of prohibited means and methods by athletes. Signing and ratifying the Convention means that the authorities of the Republic of Poland have assumed responsibility for combating doping in our country. Ladies and gentlemen! The most important provisions in the proposed bill address the fundamental issues in the fight against doping. This is the establishment of a Commission for Combating Doping in Sport as an institution competent to establish and implement disciplinary rules on combating doping in sport, including the conduct of disciplinary proceedings. To date, these obligations have been fulfilled in different ways by Polish sports unions. Very often in disciplinary proceedings conducted by unions there have been irregularities, including penalties in a way that is grossly contrary to the provisions of their own disciplinary rules and the World Anti-Doping Code. After the amendment of the rules, Polish unions will be obliged to recognise and respect the disciplinary rules already established by the committee, which will also be responsible for implementing disciplinary responsibility for doping in sport. It was also necessary, ladies and gentlemen, to update the definition of doping in sport according to the most recent definition contained in the World Anti-Doping Code, which sets international standards in this respect. Therefore, the draft law defines the concept of doping in sport, and the proposed records are also updated and extended to recognise as a doping act the cooperation of an athlete with persons who have previously been suspended or sentenced as doping, including a trainer or a person from auxiliary staff. This is a record in the World Anti-Doping Code, which is a fallout of the loud case of the cyclist, Lance, Armstrong. The introduction of international standards for combating doping in sport into Polish legislation is nothing more than ensuring the protection of the fundamental right of athletes, players to participate in doping-free sport, and thus promoting health, fairness and equality of athletes at home and abroad, ensuring harmonised, coordinated and effective doping detection programmes, discouraging and preventing it at international and national level, expressing disapproval of all actions aimed at the use of banned substances and methods, finally reaching out to all those who fight for pure sport in line with the principle of fair play. Today's proposals are also a response to the development of new doping trends in Poland and the world, including the emergence of new substances and methods considered prohibited, and above all the fulfilment of the Polish Republic's commitment to the World Anti-Doping Agency. Achievement of compliance with the global anti-doping programme will certainly affect Poland's perception of Poland as a country actually implementing the anti-doping programme, the perception of the Polish state, Polish sports unions as reliable partners in the organisation of sporting events and competitions at national and international level. The lack of alignment of Polish law and compliance with the applicable rules laid down by WADA could result in the suspension of WADA accreditation of our anti-doping laboratory, suspension of the Commission's activities to combat Doping in Sport, which could result in a lack of possibility to organize international sports competitions in Poland or in questioning the purity of Polish sport, players of national representation, trainers, sports activists. As rapporteur for the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism, but also on behalf of applicants, with a view to the well-being of Polish sports, athletes, professional athletes, their younger colleagues, their trainers, medical staff, parents and children and young people, who are particularly vulnerable to the use of prohibited substances and to the inappropriate use of dietary supplements, I ask the ladies and gentlemen of Members to treat and adopt this draft law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u300	Dear Marshal, Minister! Honourable Members! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I am pleased to present my position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Sports Act, print no. 627. The proposal provides for the modification of certain provisions of the Act of 25 June 2010 on combating doping in sport. The proposed changes to the rules aim primarily at regulating disciplinary issues related to doping in sport. It is about adapting the Polish law to international standards, including above all to the World Anti-Doping Code. First of all, the issues of establishing and implementing disciplinary rules related to doping in sport are excluded from the powers of the Polish Sports Association. This role is taken over by the Commission for Combating Doping in Sport as an entity responsible for anti-doping control. This amendment aims at shaping national arrangements for the establishment and implementation of disciplinary rules on doping in sport in a manner consistent with the principles of the World Anti-Doping Code, to which Poland is bound by the Article of the International Convention on Doping in Sport. The current provision required the Polish sports union to establish disciplinary rules on doping, although the Commission for Combating Doping in Sport is competent for its fight against doping, which has the appropriate potential to do so and the obligation to regulate in this respect on the basis of the provisions of the World Anti-Doping Code. In the current state of law, this made it necessary to adopt the rules laid down by the committee in the internal legal systems of Polish sports unions. In practice, this has led to the situation of unjustified extension of the process of adoption by Polish sports unions of disciplinary rules concerning doping in accordance with applicable international standards. The second very important change in the bill. The bill defines the concept of doping in sport. Until now, ladies and gentlemen, we have basically considered the use of an unauthorised substance to be cheering. WADA has its definition and we will implement this definition into our bill. The proposed definition of doping in sport is based on the calculation of specific behaviours considered unacceptable. This is a significant change in quality, as this definition replaces the definition used so far both in Polish law and in other international agreements the general definition, which I have just mentioned, which referred only to the use or administration of substances and methods defined according to the appropriate list. In Poland, this list was adopted in the form of a regulation. The current definition is based on the calculation of behaviours considered as doping in sport, such as not providing the required information on the whereabouts for the purpose of anti-doping control. Ladies and gentlemen, all world-class athletes are in the so-called. ADAMS. This program talks about places of residence, places of training, places of competition or even travel time. A player for 3 months must specify the exact date of the day, in principle, by the hour where he is located. Those are the requirements of this program. Next point. The draft provides for the repeal of the provisions of the Sports Act, which constitute the right of sports entities, in particular Polish sports unions, to exercise disciplinary responsibility for the use of doping in sport to the extent specified in their regulations. Another one. The draft provides for the withdrawal of the right of the World Anti-Doping Agency to bring an action before the Arbitration Tribunal. Sports at the Polish Olympic Committee in disciplinary matters for the use of doping. In view of the above, it seems appropriate to adopt the proposed period of vacathio legis, i.e. 7 days. Ladies and gentlemen! The Civic Platform Club will support the proposed changes to the Sports Act. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The bill defines the concept of doping in sport. This definition is fully in line with the current wording of the World Anti-Doping Code, to respect the principles of which Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 obliges States Parties, including the Republic of Poland, to the International Convention against Doping in Sport. The revised World Anti-Doping Code entered into force on 1 January 2015. This code is a basic and universal document based on a global programme to combat doping in sport. The aim of the Code is to promote action to combat doping through universal harmonisation of the basic anti-doping elements. It is sufficiently precise to achieve full harmonisation on issues where unity of positions is required, but at the same time generally enough in other areas to allow flexible implementation of anti-doping rules. The Code provides a set of rules defining the rights and obligations of the player, penalties for violations of anti-doping rules and proceedings before disciplinary authorities. The draft law referred to today provides for the repeal of the provisions of the Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 6 Sports Act, which constitute the right of sports entities, in particular Polish sports unions, to exercise disciplinary responsibility for the use of doping in sport to the extent specified in their regulations. On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club at the end of my speech, I would like to quote the article. 50 points 1 and 2 of the Sports Act. I address these words to all sportsmen: Who gives a minor to a substance prohibited or uses a method prohibited within the meaning of the rules on combating doping in sport, is liable to a fine, to a sentence of imprisonment or imprisonment of up to 2 years. The same penalty shall be imposed on those who, without the knowledge of the player, give him a prohibited substance or use a prohibited method within the meaning of the rules on combating doping in sport. The Polish People's Party is in favour of the adoption of the parliamentary bill amending the Sports Act. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u307	Thank you very much. Ms Małgorzata Zwierzan will speak on behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u308	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Sports Act on combating doping in sport. The most important issue in this bill is to prevent the certification of the Polish Anti-Doping Laboratory. There is no doubt that we cannot allow a situation in which, by failing to comply with the Polish legislation established by the World Anti-Doping Agency, we are deprived of the possibility of organizing international sports competitions in our country. The contested bill implements the regulations imposed on Poland by the above mentioned organisation. It establishes the establishment of a Commission for Combating Doping in Sport, a national agency representative in the territory of the Republic of Poland and the only relevant body responsible for anti-doping control. At the same time, it deprives Polish sports unions of competence in this area. Given that this committee has the appropriate potential in this respect, and the current legislation only prolongs the implementation of international standards in force from 1 January 2015, I consider this amendment appropriate. It is also appropriate for me to repeal the rules which constitute the right of sports entities, including Polish sports unions, to exercise disciplinary responsibility for the use of doping in sport, and to delegate the powers in this respect to a specially established disciplinary panel operating at the Commission for Combating Doping in Sport. We are transforming the current anti-doping control system into the model contained in the World Anti-Doping Code. The legislators also propose to modify the definition of doping in sport. I am aware of the difficulties that this change has caused and the shortcomings of the solution adopted. However, I would like to draw attention to the relentlessness of the international organisation, which we need to adapt to in the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism, which is stressed in the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism, and to the possible consequences of our decisions. The Free and Solidarni Circle will ask for further proceedings of this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u364	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a bill aimed at implementing the proposal announced in the exposé by the Prime Minister to reduce corporate income tax from 19% to 15% for small taxpayers. This law pursues this task, which is precisely the objective of reducing taxation. Of course, this is a reduction in corporate tax from 19% to 15%. , and small taxpayers are described here as those whose revenue from sales did not exceed in the previous tax year the amount in PLN corresponding to the equivalent of 1200 thousand. Euros. What is important, this offer can be used by newly created CIT-paying companies. At the same time, the media debate also raised questions about what part of the players, like a large group of players, could benefit from this proposal. In total, taxpayers qualified as small taxpayers, i.e. those who do not just exceed this limit of 1200 000. The euro, there are 393 thousand, and those entities that have shown any income to tax in 2014 are 135 000. Therefore, it is worth remembering these two numbers. At the same time, the fiscal effect of this entire amount, i.e. the lower revenue to the state budget derived from CIT tax, is estimated at about PLN 270 million. The project also introduced some minor changes sealing the income tax system. First, a uniform definition in both tax laws, i.e. PIT and CIT, of the grounds for recognising non-residents' income as achieved in Poland. There were some doubts and this clarification is indeed intended to seal the tax system. Second, the definition of new rules for determining the income from the holding of shares or shares in companies in exchange for a non-monetary contribution. Thirdly, clarification of the conditions for the use of preferential taxation in the case of mergers and divisions of companies. Fourthly, clarification of the conditions for applying the flat-rate tax exemption at the source of interest and royalties. And the last element, the clarification of the rules on determining the cost of income in the event of the sale of the shares or shares of the acquiring or newly created company. So that the bill is presented and I am counting on the support of the High House. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u372	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On the other hand, the draft law states that the reduction in the rate is to apply to a small taxpayer. The terms "small entrepreneur" and "small taxpayer" are not the same. The term "small entrepreneur' is defined both under Union law and under national law. In the Law on the Freedom of Economic Activity in Article. 105 we read that this deadline applies to companies which employ less than 50 employees on average per year in at least one of the last two financial years and achieve an annual turnover of less than EUR 10 million. On the other hand, the term "small taxpayer' is clearly defined in the Corporate and Natural Income Tax Act, but it defines those taxable persons as entrepreneurs who have earned revenues whose equivalent did not exceed EUR 1.2 million. Thus, the proposed reduction in income tax does not even apply to micro-enterprises, because within the meaning of the Commercial Freedom Act micro-entrepreneurs are entities with a turnover of up to EUR 2 million. It cannot therefore be concluded that the proposed changes fulfil the electoral promises and provide real support for small businesses. On the basis of these provisions, small companies will certainly not become medium-sized and medium-sized, as announced by the Prime Minister in the exposé. In addition, there is nothing in the proposed act, and I quote for the Prime Minister, about a double investment write-off for entrepreneurs who will reinvest their earned money in the development of their company. That's the promise in the exposé. The Modern Posel Club already in February introduced a bill amending the Corporate Income Tax Act, print No 287, in which we really propose a reduction in corporate income tax of up to 15% for small enterprises. We're raising the depreciation limits for fixed assets from 3.5,000. Up to 10,000. PLN, as well as we propose an extension of the time in which the taxpayer can settle the tax loss from 5 to 15 years. These are the elements that could really affect the development of small businesses, the increase in domestic jobs, and thus the revenue of the budget from this tax. However, the parliamentary project The modern one was frozen in the finance committee. Today's solutions not only do not simplify tax laws, but also introduce additional bureaucratic requirements for entrepreneurs under the cover of sealing regulations, making it difficult to de facto settle income taxes. In addition, in some cases they even duplicate the recently introduced anti-tax law clause. In view of the short speaking time, I may not be referring to or discussing individual solutions, or pointing out shortcomings, but indeed today Minister Ziobro with the Prime Minister announces an increase in repressiveness towards tax offenders. This is good, because it is indeed necessary to increase these repressions for dishonest taxpayers, but under one condition: simplification of tax law, because when such a complex system of tax law is in force in Poland, the additional tightening of criminal rules is unfortunately not the way. This will make these companies, which are still in Poland, likely look for a different place to develop and will de facto affect the reduction of budget revenue. [[♪ The bell ♪]] However, despite these comments, Poselski Club The modern proposal calls for this proposal to be submitted to the relevant committee for work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u450	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Act on Access to and Sharing the Benefits of Genetic Resources creates the necessary conditions for the implementation of Regulation No 511/2004 of the European Union implementing the provisions of the Nagoya Protocol as regards user obligations in the European Union. The Nagoya Protocol introduces an international system to ensure that countries providing genetic resources, mainly developing countries, can benefit from both financial and non-financial benefits from the new use of these resources, e.g. by large pharmaceutical or cosmetic companies. On the other hand, the Protocol provides for entities complying with the provisions of countries providing genetic resources to facilitate access to these resources. Thus, the Act sets out: the characteristics and tasks of public administrations in implementing Regulation No 511, the rules and procedures for carrying out checks on the compatibility of access and use of genetic resources and traditional knowledge related to these resources, the rules on liability for infringement of Regulation No 511, the procedure for entering the collection of genetic resources into the register of collection within the Union established under that Regulation, the body controlling collections already included in the register of collections within the European Union and its competences, the rules on effective, proportionate and dissuasive sanctions, introducing administrative fines for infringements of Article. 4 and 7 of Regulation No 511. a national authority within the meaning of Article. 6 Regulation No 511, is the Minister responsible for. the environment and the tasks set out in the Act relating to the control of users and the register of the collection of genetic resources shall be entrusted to the Chief Environmental Inspector and the Provincial Environmental Inspectors. In accordance with the draft law, genetic resource users shall submit to the national authority a declaration of due diligence when applying for funding for research as well as at the final stage of the introduction of a product arising from the use of genetic resources or traditional knowledge related to such products and resources. The Chief Environmental Inspector shall draw up a user control plan. The inspections shall be carried out by the competent voivodships of environmental inspectors. The draft law also includes a delegation to be issued by the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs. the environment setting out the detailed scope of these controls. The competent authority for fine-making by administrative decision shall be the competent regional environmental inspector. The revenue from administrative fines is the revenue of the State budget. The proposed law also introduces a provision providing for a waiver of the administrative penalty and the termination of proceedings where it is shown that the infringement was caused by events or circumstances which the infringer could not have foreseen. This is a bill adopted by the Council of Ministers, it does not need to be notified. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u451	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Teresa Nolas, Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u452	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present to the High House my position on the Government's draft law on genetic resources and the sharing of the benefits of their use, Parliamentary printing No 644. The project, addressed to the Sejm as a government project on 20 June, was preceded by a public consultation with 123 entities. The aim of this project is to bring it into line with EU legislation. The proposed law concerns Regulation No 511/2014 of the European Union on the Nagoya Protocol on access to and fair sharing of the benefits of the use of genetic resources. The Protocol shall apply to all genetic resources, except those of human genetics. Genetic resources, which are the subject of our procession, are particularly important in the production of food, forestry, pharmaceutical or cosmetic industries, and also play an important role in restoring degraded ecosystems and protecting endangered species. The project introduces regulations ensuring control in Poland of genetic resources obtained from countries that have legally regulated access to these resources in accordance with the Nagoya Protocol. Poland signed this document in 2011 without ratification. The proposed bill will therefore ensure that appropriate national regulations are adopted. The draft law properly sets out the tasks of public administrations regarding the implementation of Regulation No 511, as well as the principles and procedures for determining the correct use of genetic resources. The draft makes clear the principles of liability for infringement of Regulation No 511/2014. In accordance with these provisions, the Minister for the Environment is responsible for the implementation of the Convention and he will be the national authority. The tasks of controlling the users of genetic resources were entrusted to the environmental inspection body in the project, and the various articles of the Act detailed and specified those competences as well as the supervision of the authorities assigned to them. The assessment and analysis of the meetings held with the social side and the many expert reports carried out on the Nagoya Protocol show that the potential costs outweigh the benefits of the organisation of this regulatory system. It is therefore appropriate to consider that national legislation should be kept to a minimum in order to ensure that the provisions of Regulation No 511 can be applied. Therefore, it is reasonable that the project limited itself to the creation of the necessary system for controlling the use of resources from other EU countries in Poland. On the basis of the arguments put forward by the High House and in view of the need to regulate legislation in the EU regulation, I request a positive recommendation and the referral of the bill to further parliamentary work. Thank you kindly.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u453	Thank you very much, Member. I invite Mrs Dorota Sunday, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u454	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform club vis-à-vis the draft law on access to genetic resources and sharing the benefits of their use, print No. 644. The project is intended to implement EU law. One of the objectives of the Convention on Biological Diversity, drawn up in Rio de Janeiro In 1992, to which Poland is a party, Poland is equal and fair to the sharing of benefits resulting from the use of genetic resources. In order to clarify this objective, the Nagoya Protocol to the Convention on Access to Genetic Resources and a fair and equal sharing of the benefits of the use of these resources, hereinafter referred to as the Nagoya Protocol, was adopted in 2010. This Protocol, like the Convention on Biological Diversity, applies to all genetic resources within the national jurisdiction, with the exception of human genetic resources. Genetic resources mean all genetic material having actual or potential value. Genetic material is any material containing functional units of heredity. It can come from the environment, called in situ, or collections created by man, ex situ, e.g. botanical gardens, zoos, gene banks, microbiological collections of cultures. Collections, ex situ, shall be understood as a set of samples and related information collected and stored. They are the main specialised suppliers of genetic resources used in the European Union. Collections are created, among others. to provide research material or scientific work to researchers or breeders. In addition, gene banks, which are most often run by government organisations, aim at long-term storage and constitute a reserve of genetic material in the event of loss of genetic material. Collections in the context of the Nagoya Protocol play an important role within the product control chain. The project does not provide for regulation of issues arising from the Nagoya Protocol concerning access to Polish genetic resources, as the current legislation sufficiently safeguards valuable genetic resources. The scope of the regulation is limited to the creation of the necessary system to control the use of genetic resources obtained from other countries which have introduced appropriate regulation of access to their genetic resources under national law. On the other hand, the draft law sets out the tasks and competence of public administrations in implementing Regulation No 511/2014, the rules and procedures for carrying out checks on access and use of genetic resources and the rules on liability for infringements of that Regulation. The tasks of user control and collection register within the Union indicated in the proposed act have been entrusted to the Chief Environmental Inspector and the Provincial Environmental Inspectors. The register of user checks carried out will be kept by the Chief Environmental Inspector under the existing Ekoinfonet Environmental Inspection IT System. The Act sets out the procedure for entering the collection of genetic resources in the register of collections within the Union resulting from Regulation No 511. The authority controlling the collections or parts thereof shall be the Chief Environmental Inspector. The proposed law also introduces, on the basis of the ruling of the Constitutional Court, provisions providing for the waiver of the fine and the remission of proceedings where it is shown that the infringement has occurred as a result of events or circumstances which the person committing the infringement could not prevent. The proposed law imposes additional obligations on voivodship environmental inspectors who are environmental inspection bodies. The impact assessment states that the cost of implementing the law is 1800 thousand. PLN per year for 10 years. The increased costs in 2017 are related to the creation and operation of 18 workplaces - one in the Ministry of Environment and 17 in the Environmental Protection Inspection, together with special equipment and training. It follows from the summary of the consultative observations that [[♪ The bell ♪]] there are still many points of dispute and comments which I hope we will clarify with the interested environments and recipients of this law when proceeding over it. On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I request that the project be transferred to the Environment Committee for further work.

Poland is bound by the Convention on Biological Diversity of 5 June 1992 and adopted in 2010 by the Nagoya Protocol which sets out an equal and fair sharing of the benefits of the use of genetic resources. The Nagoya Protocol has created an international system to ensure that countries providing genetic resources can benefit from both financial and non-financial benefits from the new use of these resources. The Nagoya Protocol aims to prevent practices of wealth at the expense of countries with high biodiversity. Given that genetic resources play an increasingly important role in the wider economy, mutual respect for rights is extremely important. The implementation of the Nagoya Protocol by the European Union regarding the obligations of users of genetic resources is governed by the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of Europe, and Poland as a member of the European Union is also required to implement the Protocol. By means of the draft law, Poland will meet its obligations towards the Union, creating a system to control the use of genetic resources acquired in Poland from countries that have legally regulated access to their resources in accordance with the Nagoya Protocol. The environmental minister who is responsible for the implementation of the Convention on Biological Diversity and the Nagoya Protocol shall control the fulfilment of the objectives and regulations. The contested law also includes a delegation to issue a regulation of the Minister of the Environment setting out the detailed scope of user controls, guided by the need to standardise control activities. The draft law also contains provisions that introduce administrative fines for infringements of the use of genetic resources. The law and the relevant regulations will include all details concerning not only the manner in which the penalty is imposed, but also its amount, as well as the possibility of withdrawing from it and the possibility of withdrawing proceedings against a particular object. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports further proceding the government's draft law on access to and sharing the benefits of genetic resources. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u473	Thank you very much, Minister. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u474	The proposed law is the implementation of EU Regulation No 511/2014 on the Nagoya Protocol on access to genetic resources and fair and fair sharing of the benefits of the use of these resources. That Regulation shall apply together with Implementing Regulation (EU) 2015/1866. The Nagoya Protocol is a new instrument of international law implementing the third objective of the Convention on Biological Diversity. As adopted in 2010, it is intended to ensure that countries providing genetic resources can benefit from the benefits they deserve. Genetic resources are a kind of support for natural capital and the basis for food security and also play an increasingly important role in many sectors of the economy, in particular pharmaceuticals, cosmetics and biological energy sources. They play an essential role in restoring degraded ecosystems and protecting endangered species. As a resource, the law considers materials containing functional inheritance units. These may be samples derived directly from the natural environment or man-made so-called. collections, i.e. botanical gardens, zoos, gene banks and microbiological collections of cultures. New regulations under the Nagoya Protocol may affect operators active in the production of food using biotechnology, food supplements, novel food or functional food. The greatest impact may be on producers of novel foods and novel food ingredients which, prior to 15 May 1997, were slightly used in the Community for human consumption. At present, such foods or food ingredients are subject to a Community evaluation procedure before being placed on the market, where the applicant can place the product on the market after a positive assessment. The bill introduces regulations to ensure control of the use in Poland of genetic resources acquired from countries that have legally regulated access to their genetic resources in accordance with the Nagoya Protocol. Poland signed the document in 2011 but has not yet ratified it because it wants to anticipate the adoption of the relevant national regulations. The draft law sets out the tasks and characteristics of public administrations concerning the implementation of Regulation No 511/2014, as well as the principles and modalities for carrying out checks on the use of genetic resources and traditional knowledge associated with them, acquired from countries party to the Nagoya Protocol. The principles of liability for infringement of Regulation 511/2014 are also indicated. In accordance with the draft law, the Minister for the Environment, who is responsible for the implementation of the Convention on Biological Diversity and the Nagoya Protocol, will act as a national authority. The tasks related to the control of the users of genetic resources and the register of the collection of genetic resources have been entrusted to the authorities of the Environmental Protection Inspections. According to the draft law, the Chief Environmental Inspector is to draw up a plan to control the users of genetic resources. At the same time, the Chief Environmental Inspector will forward to the Environment Minister for approval a user control plan for the following year by the end of November each year. User checks will be carried out by the competent provincial inspector. The draft law also sets out the procedure for entering the collection of genetic resources in the register of collections within the European Union. The competent voivodship environmental inspector will control the collections applying for entry in this register as well as those already placed in it.
