ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u654	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I present on behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights a report on the draft Constitutional Tribunal Act 558 and 693. The Sejm at its 20th meeting on 9 and 10 June 2016 pursuant to Article 9 of the Rules of Procedure. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred to the draft law, as well as the citizens' draft law on the Constitutional Tribunal, the parliamentary draft law on the amendment of the Constitutional Tribunal Act and the parliamentary bill on the amendment of the Constitutional Tribunal Act to the Committee on Justice for consideration. The committee met on 21 and 29 June. At its first meeting on 21 June, the committee appointed an emergency subcommittee for the examination of these draft laws, which I had the honour of chairing. The subcommittee held five meetings, four of which were substantive. Written opinions on draft laws were presented by the Supreme Court, the Supreme Administrative Court, the National Court of Justice, the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Treasury, the National Chamber of Legal Counsels, as well as the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights. As a result of the work of the subcommittee and committee, the draft law annexed to the printing report No 667 was drawn up. In view of the unexpected withdrawal by the representatives of the Committee of the Civil Project Legislative Initiative yesterday, legislative work in the Committee on Justice and Human Rights started again. At its meeting on 5 July 2016, it was reiterated that the draft Constitutional Court Act No 558 would be the basis for further legislative work. As a decisive argument it was pointed out that the draft essentially restores the provisions of the earlier Constitutional Tribunal Act of 1 August 1997. The solutions to this law are well known to the authorities of the State, including the Constitutional Court, because it was ruled on the basis of it between 1997 and 2015. It was noted that the project restores regulations whose constitutionality was not contested and which at the same time did not raise major social controversy. The Commission concluded its work on 6 July 2016. During the committee's work, the representatives of most clubs expressed the view that the new law or the amendment of the Constitutional Court Act was intended to be adopted. It has repeatedly been stressed that the new law should resolve all - or at least a significant part of it - disputes around the constitutional court, meet constitutional standards and at the same time meet social expectations regarding the legitimacy and transparency of the tribunal's activities. In view of the assumption that the project would be based on the provisions of the 1997 Constitutional Court Act, during the committee's work the project was discussed in particular about those solutions which constituted a derogation from the regulations in force until 2015. The proposals were discussed in detail, which sometimes followed the amendments formally tabled - or inspired by them - representatives of the Legislative Initiative Committee and members of the clubs: Law and Justice, Civic Platform, Modern, Kukiz All the regulations of the attached draft were systematically discussed and adopted at the Justice Committee meeting on 5 and 6 July. It should be noted that this work was almost entirely consistent with the previous systematic discussion of these regulations first in the Emergency Sub-Committee and then in the Committee on 21 and 29 June. The opinions submitted and during the discussion raised doubts about some of the institutions and regulations of the project. As a result of the discussion and the amendments tabled, some of the doubts were eliminated or mitigated. 36 amendments were adopted, including amendments concerning the election of the President and Vice-President of the Tribunal by the General Assembly, disciplinary proceedings against judges, proceedings before the Court, rules and procedures for ruling, amending, transitional, adapting and final regulations. Some amendments were editorial. Some of the amendments tabled were rejected, which was motivated by constitutional doubts or by the fact that they did not fulfil the purpose of the Act as formulated by the applicants. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights, after considering the draft law, asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law as proposed in the report of the Committee on Printing No 693. I would like to thank the ladies and gentlemen, as well as all the experts and participants in the committee's work, for their dedicated cooperation. In the event of specific questions, I shall remain at the disposal of the High House.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u656	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present my position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the draft Constitutional Tribunal Act No 558 and No 693. It is a good thing that the Committee of Justice on 5 July, as previously the Emergency Sub-Committee and the Committee of Justice on 29 June, adopted the Parliamentary bill on the Constitutional Tribunal as a base project in the work on the new Law on the Court of First Instance. It is not that it has been prepared by Members of the Law and Justice. This project has another decisive advantage: it crosses most of the disputes concerning the current Constitutional Tribunal Act of 25 June 2015. This is due to the fact that this proposal essentially restores the provisions of the earlier Constitutional Tribunal Act of 1 August 1997. Let me remind you that the constitutionality of this law was not contested and that it functioned without major social controversy for almost two decades. This is a fundamental advantage over the proposal to return to the original wording of the 2015 Act, especially if account is taken of the scale of the disputes which have been brought about by the adoption of that law. As I mentioned, although the proposal essentially restores the regulation of the previous law, on some issues it contains derogations from the provisions in force at that time. It does not resolve all the issues that are currently under discussion, but offers an opportunity to stabilise the situation and lower the political temperature of the dispute around the constitutional court. The adoption of this proposal ends a period of several months of legal uncertainty related to divergent opinions on the legal nature of the Court's decisions issued in the period from March 2016. An additional advantage is the fact that the project provides for the publication of controversial decisions of the Constitutional Tribunal issued in the period from 10 March 2016. Against the expectations of the most indisputable opposition, the proposal is made that only the President of the Tribunal, the formation or a group of three Members, and not as initially assumed, including the President and the Prosecutor General, should be able to make a request to investigate the matter in full. [[Three judges, not MPs.]] Three judges, I'm sorry. We also propose that the wording be deleted so that, in certain situations, rulings of the Tribunal are taken by a majority of 2/3 of the votes. Some proposals respond to the criticism of the solutions of the current Court Act and correspond at least to the changes expected by the various parties to the dispute. As assumed by the citizens' draft Constitutional Court Act, print No 550, also the procedated project provides the possibility to study in full the constitutionality and legality of important constitutionally and socially important laws. According to another amendment, which coincides with the proposal for a civil project under the procedure for the election of the President and Vice-President, each member of the General Assembly will be able to vote for only one candidate. We also take into account the proposal of the Polish People's Party that judges should be assigned to cases in alphabetical order. The draft also takes into account the observations made by the Group of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Civic Platform in disciplinary proceedings against the judges of the Tribunal. We believe that some proposals in other draft laws on the Constitutional Court and repeated during the committee's work could lead to some existing disputes being resolved. In particular, it was interesting to propose that the judges, whose validity of the choice exists, be put to rest. Unfortunately, implementation prevents serious and widely raised reservations regarding the constitutionality of this proposal. To conclude by discussing our position, let me thank you for the participation of all sides of the political scene, although in particular for the constructive attitude of representatives of the Kukiz clubs15 and the Polish People's Party. In conclusion: It is good that there is agreement beyond one political camp concerning the need to amend the rules governing the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal. In matters that arouse a lot of political emotions and wake them up for so long, the destructive approach is either all or nothing. Since the bill presented solves some of the important problems by acting on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I declare that the club supports further work on the project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This remark applies particularly to Members, as they themselves have said, of total opposition. I would therefore like to remind you of the key concept for the theory of negotiation, which is: the zone of potential agreement. This is a common area of expectations allowing for a mutually beneficial agreement, on a win-win basis. The draft law on the Constitutional Tribunal by Law and Justice is not free of defects, but contains important concessions. The reduction of the full composition of the tribunal to 11 judges, the adoption of the case catalogue to be considered outside the order of influence, as well as the statutory guarantee of publication in the Official Journal of the Court's decisions from 10 March to 30 June 2016 give an opportunity to restore the proper functioning of the tribunal. The project raises a number of doubts concerning, among others. the President's consent to the removal of the Judge of the Tribunal after the expiry of the mandate has been established as a result of a final disciplinary decision. This solution makes de facto the President of the Republic of Poland an additional instance in disciplinary proceedings concerning judges. Another question concerns the introduction of provisions requiring the tribunal to postpone the hearing in the absence of a prosecutor-general at a hearing at which his presence is mandatory. Despite these reservations, the draft law by Law and Justice is the best solution for the projects submitted to the High House, as it restores the Constitutional Court to a place in the Polish legal system actually defined in the Constitution. The Constitutional Tribunal cannot be established as the third Chamber of Parliament. However, I have no illusions that the adoption and entry into force of the project will resolve the conflict and will cause the tribunal to function. This is a political dispute and, I say it with regret, it can only end in December when the term of office of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal ends. This is an objective observation resulting from the observation of the activities of Mr Andrzej Rzepliński, who as the actual author of the draft law adopted by the Sejm in June 2015 currently defends this solution at all costs. ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u684	Thank you very much. According to Art. The 3th National Media Council Act is composed of five members, three of whom are elected by the Sejm and two are appointed by the President of the Republic of Poland. In view of the above, it is necessary to regulate in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm the procedure for the appointment of persons to the said body by the Sejm. [[Game in the O.R.]] [[We're about to vote.]] [[Call me, the vote's coming up. Aren't you guys getting enough?]] The Act on the National Media Council provides that the Council shall present annually until 31 March to the Sejm, the Senate, the President of the Republic of Poland, the President of the Council of Ministers and the National Council of Radio and Television, and shall make public written information about its activities in the previous year. It is therefore proposed to amend the Article. 125 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, according to which the Sejm will examine inter alia. information provided by the National Media Council on its activities. The examination of this information and the report on the activities of the National Radio and Television Council, in accordance with the intentions of the applicants, should take place no later than 3 months from the date of its service to Members. Information on the activities of the Board shall be sent to a committee which, after its consideration, shall submit to the Sejm a draft resolution on acceptance or rejection of the information. The resolution of acceptance or rejection of information, together with the comments and reservations made, shall be sent by the Marshal of the Sejm to the President of the National Media Council. The Sejm, when adopting or rejecting information about the activities of the Board, shall decide on the use of information requests. These changes also imply changes to the Article. Article 4 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2, Article The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 1). Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm consisting of adding the President of the National Media Council the power to attend meetings of the Sejm of the Republic and to take part in the sitting of the Sejm in matters falling within its scope outside the order of speakers recorded in the vote, and adding the power to consider information on the activities of the National Media Council to the Sejm of the Republic. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u812	A statement by the Members on the 110th anniversary of Maciej Kalenkiewicz's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 1 July this year We celebrated the 110th anniversary of Maciej Kalenkiewicz's birth. Kotwicz - engineer, lieutenant colonel of the Polish Army, Hubalczyk, quiet, soldier of the Home Army and commander of guerrilla forces. Maciej Kalenkiewicz was born in Vilnius. He was the son of a Member of the Polish Parliament and of Jan Kalenkiewicz's term of office. In 1924, he graduated and joined the Warsaw Officers' School of Engineering, after which he was promoted to lieutenant. He graduated from the Faculty of Land Engineering at the Warsaw University of Technology. After graduating from school, he became the commander of the platoon at the School of Squadruple Reserves in Modlin. To the Captain's degree, he was promoted at the age of 20. More studies have already stopped the outbreak of war. During the September campaign, Kalenkiewicz fought in the Suwałska Cavalry Brigade and in the 110th Ulysses regiment. In October he became Deputy Commander of the Branch of the Polish Army Henryk Two months later, he went to Paris, where he completed an officer's course of bomb squad and was assigned to the OGM headquarters in Paris. After France's defeat, he went to Great Britain, where he became the commander of the bomb squad at a school in Biggar. He was still working on the possibility of establishing air transport with Poland. In December 1941, Kalenkiewicz took a flight to his homeland and was to be thrown over it. Unfortunately, by mistake, Kotwicz was found in the territory belonging to the Reich. Together with other jumpers he was immediately arrested, but managed to escape and get to Warsaw. In the capital of Kotwicz, promoted to Major, he was assigned to the Third Operations Department of the Armed Fighting Union, the OGM, and worked on the Plan for a nationwide uprising. In 1943, he also commanded the Eastern Tape, which consisted of attacking border posts between the General Government and the Reich. In 1944. Kalenkiewicz served as an inspector of the AK command for the district of Novgorod, and then took command of the group of the Nademiańskie AK. Due to the planned offensive of the Red Army, Maciej Kalenkiewicz was included in a group of officers of the Vilnius district of the AK. The task of the group was to develop a plan for the capture of Vilnius. Kotwicz was a great supporter of Operation Ostrzy Gate. However, he could not take part in the action of conquering Vilnius, because earlier, during the fights at Iwie, he was seriously injured and had to amputate one hand. Kotwicz wanted to return to the battlefield before the wound completely healed. On 5 August 1944 he took command of the district of Nowogrodzki AK. At that time, conspiracy activities for information and promotion activities were abandoned. Kalenkiewicz was then promoted to lieutenant colonel. Interested did not get one text message with this information, because at the same time the Red Army started to liquidate Polish troops in the area where Kotwicz was staying. Kalenkiewicz's unit was attacked in Surkonts. The first attack was repelable. However, many of the fighters were wounded and during the next attack, Kotwicz's troops were finally defeated. Kalenkiewicz died with his 35 soldiers. They were all buried in a mass grave near the tomb of the January insurgents. Hello and praise the heroes. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u814	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! On July 10th, the XXIII International Music Festival of Drozdowo - Łomża 2016 is held. XXIII International Music Festival Drozdowo - Łomża 2016 is one of many cultural events of this type held cyclically in the Podlasie voivodship. This year's edition is addressed to a wide range of audiences, to take place on July 1, 2016 and will cover small and larger towns of our voivodship. The capital of the festival is Drozdowo, where the former estate of the Lutosławski family is located. From this family comes the great Polish composer of the 20th century. Witold Lutosławski, whose 100th anniversary was celebrated in 2013. It is on the premises of this property, where the Museum of Nature is presently located, that two events are taking place which constitute the buckle of the International Music Festival of Drozdowo - Łomża Days, i.e. The inaugural concert and the final concert. Most concerts will take place every year in Łomża and surrounding towns such as Jedwabne, Czyżew, Tykocin and Kolno. These places of presentation of festival concerts are important centres on the map of Poland which testify to the multiculturalism of our homeland. The Festival is a unique event that creates the possibility of wide contact with classical music, both the demanding one and the one that is used to be called: classical popular music. The performers of festival concerts are outstanding Polish and foreign artists of opera and operatic scenes, but also young artists who sometimes take their first stage steps there gain experience by performing with well-known and recognized artists. Since the beginning of the festival, it has been the intention of organizers to present endless values of classical music, facilitate access to this music and encourage listeners to receive it. The repertoire of each edition of the festival is complemented by special concerts addressed to a wider audience, presenting hits from the most famous musicals and films, ethnic and improvised music, including traditional jazz, but also jazz classical music arrangements. The festival gathers around its ideas of people of art, cultural animators, volunteers, inhabitants of the region, builds a rich network of local partnerships and what is important promotes the culture and cultural value of the small homeland both in Poland and beyond. The event is free for the public. Artists travel to small localities to share their talents with people whose access to high culture is very limited. I cordially invite you on behalf of performers, organizers and sponsors, wishing you an unforgettable artistic experience, to XXIII International Music Festival Drozdowo - Łomża 2016.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-07-06-sejm-22-2.u816	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This year, the 400th anniversary of the town's location is celebrated in Nowy Wiśnicz. I am honored to be able to look with you at the beautiful history of this city when I hold the seat of a Member of the Polish Parliament. This extraordinary town is filled with history and magnificent monuments, from a powerful castle beginning, on wooden, roadside figures to the end. He owes his uprising to Lubomirski, who in the first half. The 17th century made Wiśnicz their native nest. The first owners of these lands were the mighty Gryphy family. Probably at the beginning of the 14th century, Wiśnicz got into the hands of the knight's family Kmithy of the Crest of Szreniawa. The apogee of the greatness of the family fell on the I half. The 16th century, when Piotr V Kmita, Marshal of the Crown and the Cracow Voivode, was the master of Wiśnicz, one of the richest and most influential people in Poland. After the death of the last of Kmita Wiśnicz after a few years of family disputes, he passed into the hands of Barzów, who seriously owed the cherry goods. In 1593. Stanisław Barzy sold Wiśnicz Castle and its goods to Sebastian Lubomirski. The capture of Wiśnicz by Stanisław Lubomirski started a period of greatness of the village. First of all, Lubomirski led to the establishment under the castle of the city, which was later called Nowy Wiśnicz. It happened on June 8, 1616, when King Zygmunt III Vasa granted the city a locality privilege in which he allowed three annual fairs and warehouses for goods coming from Hungary - wine, copper, iron. For the better development of trade in this city, the king released the cherry merchants from paying customs throughout the Crown. In 1620 in the city the parish was erected, which a few years later received a magnificent Baroque church founded by the voivode. The Assumptions of the Blessed Virgin Mary and St. John the Baptist. Wiśnicz's period of glory passed with the death of Stanisław Lubomirski in 1649. Neither his son Alexander, nor Joseph's grandson, nor the next owners treated Wiśnicz as an ancestral residence. In 1783, Wiśnicz suffered another loss - the Austrian Emperor Joseph II destroyed the monastery of the barefoot Carmelites, and the monastery buildings were converted into a heavy prison and a criminal court. The biggest blow to the city, however, was a huge fire in 1863, which consumed almost the entire wooden building of New Wiśnicz. How beautiful these were houses, we can see today, watching drawings by Jan Matejka, who was a frequent guest in Wiśnicz, because from here his wife Theodora from Giebultowskie came from. Today Nowy Wiśnicz is an important cultural, economic and historical centre of the Lesser Poland voivodship. 400th anniversary of the New Year's Uprising Wiśnicza is not only a celebration of the city, but above all a celebration of all its inhabitants, because it is they who create history, multiculturalism and richness of the city. That's what's going on in New York. Wiśnicz was born a great painter Juliusz Kossak, and more or less connected with this city such artists as Jan Matejko, Stanisław Klimowski, Stanisław Nowak, Marian Rojek or Jan Brzękowski. On the occasion of my birthday, I would like to pass on to the local authorities of New Wiśnicz and all its residents the wishes of the next successful years of development. May every decision made best serve the city. I wish you only exceptional days, and the anniversary will be an inspiration to the next unique challenges.
